## 2026-09-02 · The worker pay invoice raises itself, per stage, the moment the client approves; a founder-set default when there's no pack to read

**Founder's correction to the same-session feature above: not one invoice at the end, one per stage, and not an admin's errand to remember.** "It should not be us deciding what goes in, it should be the worker and client payment terms that are being raised against," her own words. `raise_job_stage_worker_pay_invoice(job, stage)` reads a stage's own `proportion_percent` from whichever pack governs the job, same document and same priority order as the completion trigger (`20260902h`): an approved Kickoff Pack first, an approved Quote Pack draft second. `trg_raise_worker_pay_on_stage_approval`, `after insert on stage_approvals`, calls it the instant `_do_approve_stage()` inserts the client's own approval, whatever channel that approval came through, portal, WhatsApp, in person. A failure to raise (no pack yet found, some data problem) is logged and swallowed inside the trigger, never allowed to block the approval itself: the client's evidence sign-off is the consequential act this whole feature hangs off, the invoice is bookkeeping that follows it, not a precondition on it.

**Materials are never split by proportion, and never assumed present.** "The material is dependent on the job as some have it, some don't," founder's own words, confirming a design already reached independently: whether a stage's invoice carries materials is read off whether a `kind = 'materials'` evidence row actually exists for that stage, a fact, not a guess.

**A founder-set default for a job with no pack at all: 25% on the first stage, the rest on the second, nothing past that.** Not this session's invention: "if there is not payment term, it is a 25% and the rest split," stated with the same authority as every other split in this codebase. `invoices.notes` says plainly when a stage used the default rather than an agreed document, so nobody mistakes Yaadly's fallback for something the worker and client actually signed.

**Reuses `invoices.stage`, already present and otherwise retired** since the old per-stage agency-fee invoice was dropped (`20260901y`): here it names which stage of the *worker's* pay this invoice is for, distinguished from the whole-job invoice (`20260902i`, `stage` null) by `payable_to` plus a populated `stage`.

**A real gap closed in the same pass, not left for later.** A trigger-raised invoice lands as `draft`, and nothing in the desk's Job Invoices view could actually send a draft that hadn't come through its own "Raise & send" click, it would have sat there forever with no path to being emailed. Added a plain **Send** action for any `draft` invoice, same `fn({action:"send"...})` call the existing raise flow already makes.

**A deliberate limit, stated rather than silently decided: raising is automatic, sending is not.** Every other invoice in this codebase needs a human's click to actually email a client, matching the governing rule. Auto-raising a correctly computed draft the moment the client approves satisfies "raised against the payment terms" without also making this the first thing in the system that emails real money figures to a real client with nobody having looked at it. Flagged to the founder rather than assumed either way.

**Verified live**, all three paths, each via a real function or trigger call, none left standing: `raise_job_stage_worker_pay_invoice('JOB-TEST-WAPAY-3', 1)` and `(..., 2)` against its real Quote Pack (30%/70% of J$8,000 labour) came back J$2,400 and J$5,600, summing to the full labour price. The trigger itself, fired by a real `insert into stage_approvals`, in a rolled-back transaction, correctly raised against both an approved Kickoff Pack (`JOB-TEST-KICKOFF-1`, "Mobilization and assessment," J$1,300) and, separately, the no-pack default (`JOB-TEST-REALPHOTO`, a real job with no pack, J$2,500 then J$7,500 off J$10,000 labour, stage 3 correctly raising nothing). `pg_trigger` confirms it live and enabled on `stage_approvals`.

## 2026-09-02 · A second, separate invoice: what the client owes the worker, raised as a record only

**Founder's own instruction, direct: Yaadly raises two invoices per booked job, not one, paid separately, to different parties.** The agency fee invoice (20260901y) already existed: Yaadly's own 15%, billed to and paid by the client, to Yaadly. Nothing anywhere raised the second one, a record of what the client owes the worker, labour plus materials at cost. `20260901v`'s own comment already said the client pays the worker directly; this gives that arrangement paperwork instead of leaving it entirely informal.

**Not payment integration, and checked against CLAUDE.md 9 before building it.** No money moves through Yaadly on this invoice, no provider, no holding structure, no release condition changes: the client still pays the worker exactly as before, off-platform, on their own terms. It reuses the existing `invoices`/`invoice_lines` machinery and its existing guards (`invoice_line_price_guard`, `invoice_status_guard`) unchanged, admin-raised and admin-marked-paid exactly like the agency fee invoice already is. `payable_to` (`'yaadly' | 'worker'`, default `'yaadly'`) is the only new column, so every invoice raised before today keeps its existing meaning.

**Gated on the job actually being `complete`.** Raising a bill for work not yet finished and approved would be inviting a client to pay before the evidence exists to back it; `raise_job_worker_pay_invoice()` refuses otherwise. One per job, same discipline as the agency fee.

**A real mistake caught before it shipped, not after.** The rendered invoice document's footer prints `app_settings.invoice_pay_to`, Yaadly's own bank details, unconditionally, on every invoice, because until today every invoice was Yaadly's own. Left as is, a worker-pay invoice would have told a client to pay a bill for the worker's labour into Yaadly's bank account, the exact confusion this whole feature exists to prevent. `renderInvoice()` in `yaad-invoice` now branches on `payable_to`: a worker-pay invoice's footer says plainly that Yaadly is not the payee and does not hold the money, instead of showing bank details at all. Caught by reading the render function fully before wiring the desk button to it, not by testing the button and noticing the output looked wrong.

**Verified live.** `raise_job_worker_pay_invoice('JOB-TEST-WAPAY-3')` raised `INV-2026-0008`, J$9,500 (labour J$8,000 + materials J$1,500, no fee added), sent, and its rendered footer read back with the corrected worker-pay text, not Yaadly's bank details. `deno check` clean on `yaad-invoice`, deployed from disk with `verify_jwt` read first and left at its existing `true`.

**Not committed: `concierge/concierge.html`.** Already carried substantial unrelated uncommitted work before this session touched it (101 lines changed once combined). The new Job Invoices UI, two rows per job instead of one, is verified live in the local desk; `supabase/functions/yaad-invoice/index.ts` and the new migration are committed, `concierge.html` is left as a working-tree change for whoever owns the rest of it.

## 2026-09-02 · The same fix, reapplied on top of a second session's newer work; the portal's own stage display gets it too

**Two sessions on this branch at once, `sync_job_status()` changed under this one mid-task.** Minutes after `20260902g` (below) shipped, the live function had already moved on: a fee-paid-first gate (`if not fee_paid then new.status := 'awaiting_payment'; return new;`, ahead of what that migration's own file on disk shows) and `coalesce(final_stage_count, 1)` in place of `5 - 1`, neither of which this session wrote. The Quote Pack fallback `g` added had been silently dropped, reverted to reading `kickoff_packs` alone. Nobody's change was wrong on its own; they landed on top of each other. `20260902h` re-adds only the fallback, inside the structure actually live at the time (re-read via `pg_get_functiondef`, not assumed from the last file this session wrote), the fee-paid gate and its own default left exactly as found.

**`JOB-TEST-WAPAY-3` correctly went back to `awaiting_payment` after this**, not a regression: its agency fee invoice was never actually marked paid, and the new gate is a real, separate rule that has nothing to do with either pack. Confirmed the two fixes are independent before concluding that.

**The portal's own "Stage X of Y" never read either pack either**, a second, cosmetic instance of the same root cause: `stageCount` in `web/app/portal/(gated)/jobs/[id]/page.tsx` was `Math.max(job.stage, ...evidence stages seen, 1)`, "the highest stage number observed so far," coincidentally readable as a real total but never actually one. `web/lib/portal/journey.ts` gained `quotePackPaymentStages()`, the same shape as `packPaymentStages()` for the other document's `docs.payment_stages`. The page now tries an approved Kickoff Pack first, then an approved Quote Pack draft, same order and same reasoning as the trigger, so the portal's own number agrees with the one that actually decides completion. `currentPackStage`, the client's per-stage amount-due figure, was quietly reading kickoff-only too and is fixed the same way.

**Not committed.** `page.tsx` and `journey.ts` both already carried substantial uncommitted work from the other session before this edit (163 and 21 lines respectively once combined). Committing either now would sweep that work in under this session's name. `web`'s own `tsc --noEmit` is clean and the fix is verified live in the dev server; the two files are left as working-tree changes for whoever owns that other work to fold in or commit themselves.

## 2026-09-02 · The completion trigger reads a Quote Pack's stage count too, not only a Kickoff Pack's

**Found live while manually testing the worker journey end to end, join through paid, not caused by that test.** `sync_job_status()`'s completion check (`is_complete := stage > final_stage_count`) only ever looked up `final_stage_count` from an approved `kickoff_packs` row. `20260902c`/`d`, built the same day, added an entirely separate Quote Pack path (`quote_pack_drafts`) as an alternative to the Kickoff Pack, not a variant of it, so a job that goes through Quote Pack confirmation never gets a `kickoff_packs` row at all. The lookup came back null and silently fell back to the generic default of 4 remaining stages, whatever the Quote Pack itself had actually promised the client.

**Confirmed live, on a real job.** `JOB-TEST-WAPAY-3`'s approved Quote Pack promised 2 payment stages (`docs->'payment_stages'`, 30%/70%); the portal's own progress bar, reading a third, independent count, was showing "Stage 3 of 3"; the trigger, by its own math, would not have completed the job until stage 5. All three disagreed with each other, and the client's own evidence approvals on stages 1 and 2 correctly released each stage's money regardless, since stage-level release never depended on the total count. Only the job's final `complete` flip, and with it the worker's own "how were you paid" screen, was stuck behind the wrong number.

**Fix.** `sync_job_status()` now checks `kickoff_packs` first, exactly as before, and only if that comes back null, checks `quote_pack_drafts` for an approved row and reads its stage count from `docs->'payment_stages'` directly (a plain array, not nested under `payment_schedule` the way a Kickoff Pack stores it, the two documents were never the same shape). Since a job can only ever go through one of the two paths (`20260902d`'s own reasoning), the coalesce always lands on whichever document the job actually has, never a guess between them. A one-line `update jobs set updated_at = now()` for every job sitting on an already-approved Quote Pack draft was included in the same migration, so an already-stuck job like `JOB-TEST-WAPAY-3` didn't have to wait for an unrelated write to notice the fix.

**Verified live.** Before the fix: `JOB-TEST-WAPAY-3` at `status = 'in_progress'`, `stage = 3`. Applied `20260902g_completion_trigger_reads_quote_pack_stage_count_too.sql` directly to `leffyisvfvjwzilydlwf`. After: the same job, no further action taken on it, read back as `status = 'complete'`. Continued the manual test through to the worker actually recording `pay_method = 'bank_transfer'` via the real portal form, the one screen this whole path exists to reach.

## 2026-09-01 · A worker's text or voice reply, no photo attached, now produces a report the same way a photo does

**The second half of the founder's own instruction on the report pipeline** (the daily prompt is the entry above this one): a worker's reply to it, most days a voice note or a couple of words with nothing to photograph, had nowhere to go. `yaad-inbound` had no lane that read a worker's own freeform update as anything at all: it either matched one of the existing narrow lanes (confirming a drafted report, answering a client's specific comment, a Kickoff Pack code) or fell all the way through into the client job-intake pipeline, read as a stranger describing a brand new problem.

**No new pipeline. A door into the one that already existed.** `composeEvidenceReport` (`yaad-notify-client`) already reads `evidence.label` text for a stage, regardless of whether the row carries a photo, built that way originally so photo captions and voice-note transcripts could share one composer. `schedule_evidence_landed_notify()` already fires on any insert into `evidence`, not only one with `storage_path` set. The gap was never in the composing or the notifying, only in `yaad-inbound` never actually filing a text-only or voice-only update as an `evidence` row in the first place. Filing it is the whole change: a new lane, last in line inside the WhatsApp block (after every more specific worker and client lane has had first claim, before the client-intake fallback), that transcribes a voice note locally if there is one, resolves the phone to a worker with active jobs the same way the comment-reply lane already does, and inserts straight into `evidence` with `label` set to the text and every file-specific column left null, same shape the portal and concierge desk already render as "filed without an image." More than one active job asks for the job's code first, reusing the exact prompt and session shape the evidence-photo lane already uses for the same question.

**A real discrepancy found and resolved while verifying this, not caused by it.** Confirming that a text-only row would actually reach a client meant tracing what sets `jobs.status = 'evidence'`, since `due_evidence_landed_notifies()`'s own `should_notify` check depends on it. The trigger bound to `evidence` inserts, `trg_evidence_marks_awaiting_approval`, currently executes `poke_job_on_evidence_insert()`, a function that only touches `jobs.updated_at`, not status, which read at first like the status flip had quietly stopped working for everything, not only for a text-only row. Reading `20260831g_evidence_status_needed_one_authority.sql` resolved it: this is the documented, intentional fix for an earlier version of this same trigger that raced against `sync_job_status()` and lost. The poke's only job is to touch `jobs.updated_at` so `sync_job_status()`, a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger on `jobs` itself, recomputes status from scratch, including a check for unapproved evidence against the stage being worked, a check that has never cared whether the evidence row carries a file. Confirmed live, in a rolled-back transaction: a text-only evidence row against a job at `status = 'in_progress'` correctly recomputed it to `status = 'evidence'` in the same statement.

**Verified live** through the layer that can be verified without a real signed WhatsApp message: `deno check` clean across every Edge Function, not only the one touched; the four Deno test files under `yaad-inbound/` (36 tests, including the ones that scan `index.ts`'s own source to confirm it imports `pickJobChoice`, `pickEvidenceItem` and the signature check rather than growing a second copy) all pass unchanged; a text-only `evidence` insert in a rolled-back transaction correctly scheduled an `evidence_landed_pending` timer and correctly flipped `jobs.status` to `evidence`. **Not verified**: `yaad-inbound` actually recognising a real, Twilio-signed WhatsApp message as this lane, which needs `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN`'s real signature, held by Twilio and the deployed function only, never by this session. Deployed to `leffyisvfvjwzilydlwf` with `--no-verify-jwt` preserved (`yaad-inbound`'s live setting was read first, not assumed, before deploying). See `RUNBOOK.md` for the one real WhatsApp message that finishes proving this.

---

## 2026-09-01 · A daily prompt asks every worker on a live job for a report, deliberately separate from the silence nudge

**Founder's instruction: every live job with a worker attached should be asked, once a day, for a follow-up report, voice note or a couple of words and pictures, and the report pipeline needs an agent behind it.** Two pieces; this entry is the daily prompt only. Built as its own table (`daily_checkin_log`) and its own cron-driven function (`yaad-daily-checkin`), rather than a shorter clock bolted onto `yaad-job-health`: that function watches for silence and only speaks after three days of it, this one asks every day regardless of silence, because the daily ask is the point, not a symptom of the job having gone quiet. Same reasoning `job_followups` already gave for not inventing a second shape where only the trigger differs.

**Always sends through an approved WhatsApp Content Template, never free text, no exceptions.** A daily, scheduled ping is business-initiated by definition every single time, so it cannot rely on the 24 hour customer-service window the reactive notifications sometimes get to use. `TWILIO_CONTENT_SID_DAILY_CHECKIN` is not set yet: submitting a template for Meta's approval is a Twilio console action, outside what a migration or an agent can do on its own, and outside what should be decided without the founder seeing the exact wording going out under her business's WhatsApp identity. Until that secret exists, the function says so plainly on every run rather than quietly sending nothing and reporting success; see `RUNBOOK.md` for the exact template text proposed and the steps to bring it live.

**A mistake made and caught before it could do any damage.** Verifying the auth path meant temporarily overwriting `app_settings.daily_checkin_cron_secret_sha256` with a known test value's hash to prove the authorised path responds correctly, then restoring it, matching the discipline this file already keeps for test data. The restore used a freshly generated random value instead of reading back the plaintext actually baked into the live `cron.job` command, which meant the hash and the secret the 21:00 UTC cron would actually present no longer matched, the same class of mistake `20260831zz`'s entry describes catching in a different function's secret handling. Caught immediately by re-checking `cron.job.command` directly rather than assuming the fix had worked, and corrected the same way `20260831m` reads an existing secret back out of a live function body: the real plaintext extracted from the cron job's own stored command, rehashed, and set back. Re-verified live afterward, 200 and the correct honest response, before moving on.

**Verified live**, the function deployed from disk and called with a wrong secret (403, `Not authorised`) and with the real one (200, the honest "not configured" response), rather than trusting the deploy output alone. The cron job itself was confirmed scheduled (`0 21 * * *`, active) directly against `cron.job`, not assumed from the migration having applied cleanly.

**What this does not do, on purpose.** A worker's reply to this prompt lands back through `yaad-inbound` exactly like any other WhatsApp message; wiring that reply, text or voice with no photo attached, into the same draft/confirm/relay loop evidence already gets through `composeEvidenceReport` is the next piece, not built here. Until it is, a worker who replies to this daily prompt with words alone has nowhere for that reply to go yet.

## 2026-09-01 · Arrival check-in captures one GPS point, checked against the job's parish, not its address

**Founder's instruction: the "I'm on site" tap should geotag, so it can show a worker is actually there.** Weighed directly against a promise not yet published anywhere live but already decided for the worker recruitment one-pager, "no tracking." Resolved as: build it, and when that one-pager copy is written, its wording carries the honest version, a one-time site check on tap, not continuous tracking, rather than the two standing side by side saying opposite things. Grepped first to confirm the promise is not yet in any shipped page or component; nothing published needed rewriting today.

**One reading, not a trail.** `log_arrival()` (`20260901za`) already refuses a second check-in on the same stage the same Jamaica-local day, so the ceiling is one coordinate per stage per day, structurally, not by convention. The browser's geolocation prompt is the consent moment; the check-in itself is never gated on saying yes to it, matching the in-person-confirmation checkbox already on the Approve button, evidence that strengthens a record without becoming a precondition for it.

**Checked against the job's `parish`, not `jobs.addr`.** The address column is free text nobody has ever geocoded, and geocoding it would mean sending a client's address to a mapping API, a new third party touching personal data, which CLAUDE.md reserves as Monique's own call, not a default an agent reaches for mid-build. `parish` is already a column on the job, and Jamaica has 14 of them; their town centroids are public, static geography, no vendor, no API call, no data leaving Postgres. `normalize_parish()` folds the live spelling variants (`St Andrew`, `St. Andrew`, `Saint Catherine`, `Portmore (St. Catherine)`) down to one key first, since `jobs.parish` has never had one canonical form. The flag (`arrival_log.far_from_site`, true past 30km) is a coarse sanity check, not pinpoint verification, and is never shown to the worker or the client, never refuses anything, and nothing reads it automatically: same shape as `worker_stall_history`, a fact for a human to glance at.

**A mistake made and caught in the same session.** The first pass tried to widen `log_arrival(text)` to four parameters with `CREATE OR REPLACE`, which cannot change a function's argument list; Postgres silently created a second, overloaded function instead of replacing the first, leaving the old one-argument version live with its old body. Caught by checking `pg_proc` directly rather than trusting the migration's own comment, fixed in the same session (`20260901zb`) by dropping the stale overload.

**Verified live**, not by reading the migration and assuming: a real job, three coordinate scenarios run through `log_arrival()` inside a transaction and rolled back rather than left as test rows, a point 1km from its parish centroid came back `far_from_site = false`, a point 127km away (Montego Bay coordinates against a St Andrew job) came back `true`, and a same-day, same-stage repeat call with no coordinates was correctly absorbed as `already_logged_today` without a second row. `web/`'s own typecheck and its 47-test suite both pass with `ArrivalCheckIn.tsx` converted to a client component. **Not yet deployed**: `web/` changes were verified locally; per the standing gap this file has already named once, nothing in this session's workflow deploys `web/` on its own, that step is still open.

## 2026-09-01 · No agency fee invoice is ever split by milestone, jobs or services

**Founder's instruction, replacing what the entry above logged as undesigned, same session: "It does not take 15% on milestone payments or payment terms, it calculates the full price of each job and service and takes the payment from there."** Applies to both mechanisms that raise Yaadly's own fee, never the worker's own pay in either case.

**Jobs.** `raise_job_stage_invoice()`, per-stage, evidence-gated, is dropped outright. `raise_job_agency_fee_invoice(p_job)` replaces it: one invoice per job, 15% of the whole accepted quote's labour price, raisable as soon as the job has an accepted quote (which, per `choose_worker()`, is also the same moment its Kickoff Pack is approved and the job starts), gated on nothing else. Identified in `invoices` by `job_id is not null and stage is null`. The Job Invoices desk view and the Agency Fees "ready to raise" list were both rewritten to one row per job instead of one row per stage. This is Yaadly's own cut only; the client's own payment schedule to the worker, stage by stage against evidence, in the job's Kickoff Pack, is untouched, a separate arrangement this decision has no opinion on.

**Services.** `raise_service_invoice()`'s deposit/balance split for anything £200 and over is retired; every one-off service now raises exactly one invoice for its full published price, before work starts, whatever the price. The monthly retainer branch was already one invoice and needed no change. The public payment terms page (`docs/payments.html`) and the concierge desk's own hint text next to the raise button, which named the £200 rule directly, were both updated to match, since the desk explicitly promises to follow what that page says. **The `docs/payments.html` change is not live yet**, GitHub Pages serves what's on `main`, and this session made no commit.

## 2026-09-01 · A new Agency Fees view, one place both kinds of Yaadly-only invoice live

**Founder's instruction: a channel for Yaadly's own invoices and fees, taken from every job and every service, so nothing owed to it is ever sitting uncollected on a screen she didn't happen to open.** Every row in `invoices` already meets that description; the worker's own pay never runs through this table on any path. What was missing was one place both kinds sat together: a job's per-stage fee (Job Invoices) and a drafted service or retainer (Invoices) were only ever visible on their own separate screen.

## 2026-09-01 · A worker's own quote became an AI-drafted, worker-edited pack, not four bare numbers

Founder's own correction, live: what she'd built to fire the moment a client accepts a quote was drafting the big 12-section Kickoff Pack, which was wrong. Her actual model: the moment a job goes live, a small agent drafts a short overview of the job (scope, what's included and excluded, a rough timeline, payment-stage structure), before any worker has looked at it. A worker considering the job opens the quote form to that draft instead of a blank one, edits it to their own terms, drops their price in, and what they send IS their quote, scope, timeline, stages and all, not just a labour figure. The big Kickoff Pack is untouched: it still fires at `kickoff_requested`, against whichever quote(s) the client has accepted by then, unchanged.

**Same two absolute rules as the big pack, carried over rather than re-derived:** never a price, cost or amount in anything the agent writes (Monique sells oversight judgement, not pricing, CLAUDE.md §5), never the word escrow (CLAUDE.md §6, §8). `yaad-quote-pack`'s SYSTEM prompt states both directly, and the same `guardrails.scan()` the big pack runs is run here before a draft is ever marked `ready`; a dirty draft simply never reaches `usableDraft()` in `QuotePanel.tsx`, which falls back to blank fields rather than showing a worker a document that leaked a price into a live quote.

**One draft per JOB, not per worker.** The scope and timeline come from the job intake, which does not change depending on who reads it. `quote_pack_drafts` (`20260901l`) mirrors `kickoff_drafts`' shape deliberately, much smaller: no evidence checklist, no risk register, none of the big pack's twelve sections. `yaad-quote-pack-check` (`20260901m`, cron, once a minute) polls `jobs` for exactly `open_jobs`' own definition of live (`open = true`, unassigned, `stage = 0`) and requests one draft per job with no draft yet.

**Founder's own follow-up correction, same session, after reading the first live draft:** "high level of what is included and excluded needs to be added to this, so each is stating exactly what they are doing and not doing." `included` and `excluded` (short bullet lists, high level, 2 to 5 items each) added to the SYSTEM prompt and the JSON shape (`20260901o` for storage, `20260901p` for `quotes_for_code()`), rendered in `QuotePanel.tsx` as two more editable text fields alongside scope, timeline and payment stages, and shown to the client on both the code-bearer quotes page and the signed-in portal job page. Every one of these fields is plain text, one item per line where it's a list: the founder's own description throughout was "editable text", never a structured form, so nothing here is stored as jsonb the worker would need a form to edit.

**Verified live, twice** (once before the included/excluded addition, once after): a real test job inserted, the cron caught it within its own one-minute interval, a draft came back `ready` in under 30 seconds both times, guardrail-clean, payment stage percentages summing to 100, included/excluded distinct from each other and concrete to the test job rather than generic. Test job and draft deleted immediately after each check; nothing fake stayed in the database.

**Left for later, not decided here:** no admin-desk view onto `quote_pack_drafts` yet, unlike the big pack's own Kickoff Drafts view. A dirty or stuck draft is currently only visible by querying the table directly (see RUNBOOK); a worker just sees blank fields instead of a starting point, which degrades gracefully but silently.

## 2026-09-01 · Job Invoices: Yaadly's own 15% fee, per stage, raised straight off the job's own Kickoff Pack

Follow-on to the mockup the founder reviewed the same evening, approved, and asked to be built for real. **Not the worker's cut.** Asked directly how the worker's own 12% gets collected, given the client pays the worker direct and Yaadly holds no money: the honest answer was that nothing collects it today, and would need either payment holding (out of scope until legal and insurance clear it) or a Founder Pro waiver. The founder chose the waiver, live: workers are not charged their fee until full launch, told so directly, closing that question without touching payment infrastructure. Only the client-side 15% Guarantee & Support fee is built here.

**Two schema changes to the existing `invoices` table, both from `20260826_invoicing.sql`, not a new system.** `currency` gains `'JMD'` alongside `'GBP'`: a marketplace job's own price is JMD, and converting it to GBP at raise time would mean inventing an FX rate and a rate policy this was not the place to decide. `invoices` gains `stage`, set only on a job-stage fee invoice, so "has this stage already been raised" is an exact check rather than a guess from free text.

**`raise_job_stage_invoice(job, stage)`** reads the job's own approved Kickoff Pack for that stage's name and `proportion_percent`, and the accepted quote's `labour_jmd`; the fee is `15% × stage% × labour_jmd`, `price_source = 'manual'` for the same reason `raise_service_invoice()`'s split halves used it - a policy-computed amount, not the model's. Refuses outright if the stage has not actually been approved (`stage_approvals`), matching the founder's own words: "it would be actioned once the evidence is received." Refuses again if that stage already has a live invoice, so raising twice cannot double-bill. `yaad-invoice`'s `money()` and `renderInvoice()` learned to read `invoices.currency` rather than assuming GBP everywhere, the same fix `raise_service_invoice` never needed since it never left GBP.

**The concierge desk's new Job Invoices view** mirrors the mockup card for card: one job, one strip of raised/ready/waiting totals, one row per stage with its release condition, its computed fee, and a "Raise & send" button that only lights up once that stage is genuinely approved. One click raises it, renders it, emails it, and marks it sent - the same one-click shape `raise_service_invoice` already had, extended to jobs.

Verified against real data, not assumed: `JOB-TEST-REAL-WA`'s own pack (30%/70%, labour J$5,000) raised stage 1 at exactly J$225 (15% of 30% of J$5,000); stage 2 correctly refused as not yet approved; raising stage 1 a second time correctly refused as already raised. Deployed to `concierge.yaadly.co.uk` the same evening, `concierge-deploy`'s own copy-then-deploy step, not skipped.

---

## 2026-09-01 · Raising a service invoice is one click: read the terms, price it, email it, mark it sent

Founder's own correction to the same evening's `raise_service_invoice()`: "if there is a hundred jobs, I have to manually process a hundred invoices, no." The RPC already read the correct price and term shape off the catalogue; what it still needed was to actually reach the client, so raising and sending stayed two separate manual steps.

**A third term shape, found the same conversation.** The published terms name three: under £200 in full, £200 and over split 50/50, and "the Oversight Retainer is billed monthly, and you can cancel with 30 days notice." `raise_service_invoice()` only knew the first two - a retainer would have gone through the £200 split branch and come out as a one-off deposit and balance, wrong for a recurring service. `service_catalogue.recurring` already says which items this applies to, so the fix asks nothing new of an admin: a recurring item now raises exactly one invoice for the period (current month by default), never split, its notes naming the cancellation term instead of the £200 rule.

**`yaad-invoice` gained a `send` action.** Renders the exact document the client will read (the same `renderInvoice()` the manual "render" action already used), emails it via Resend, and only on a successful send moves the invoice from `draft` to `sent` - a failed send leaves it a draft rather than claiming something went out that did not. Deliberately refused for an `ai`-drafted invoice: those still need a human to read the model's proposed lines first, the same gate the free-text drafting flow's own separate "mark sent" click already enforces. A policy-raised invoice carries no model-written content at all, only a catalogue name, a policy-computed amount, and a fixed sentence naming which published rule it follows, so there is nothing left for a human to check that raising it did not already guarantee.

**Concierge's "Raise, per your own published terms" became "Raise & send."** One click now: raise (one or two invoices, per the term shape) and send each by email, no separate step. Verified the raising half again against real catalogue prices after the retainer fix; the send half is built and deployed but not yet exercised end to end - it needs a real admin browser session with `RESEND_API_KEY` configured, which is the founder's to run, not something a database-level test can stand in for.

**A client-facing invoice list also reached the portal**, on the same `/portal/services/[id]` page as the payment terms themselves: every non-draft invoice for that service, its status, its notes stating which term applies, and its amount. RLS (`invoices_client_read`, already in the schema since `20260826_invoicing.sql`) already permitted a client to read their own non-draft invoices; nothing in the portal had ever queried the table before tonight.

## 2026-09-01 · The published payment terms reached the portal, and invoices finally follow the £200 split they promise

Founder's own instruction, live: "the payment terms should be on the portal once it is agreed and the invoices should be raised to align with this." The terms in question already exist, word for word, on `docs/payments.html` - bank transfer only, GBP/USD/CAD, account details never change mid-job, reports under £200 paid in full before work starts, £200 and over is half before and half on delivery. Two real gaps: a client who had actually booked a service could not read any of this inside their own portal room, and nothing in `yaad-invoice`'s drafting flow knew the £200 rule existed - an admin types free text, the AI proposes catalogue lines, and the split only happens if the admin remembers to type two invoices.

**Portal**: `/portal/services/[id]/page.tsx` gained the same section verbatim, with a comment noting it has to be kept in sync with the marketing page by hand - there is no shared source between the static site and this app to drift-check automatically.

**Invoicing**: `raise_service_invoice()` (`20260901t`), a second door onto the existing `invoices`/`invoice_lines` tables from `20260826_invoicing.sql`, not a new system. Reads the real price from `service_catalogue`, never a typed number, and raises one invoice under £200 (full amount, `catalogue_full`, due before work starts) or two at £200 and over (50/50, `price_source = 'manual'` for the halves - a policy-computed amount is genuinely a human decision, not the model's, the same reasoning the schema already uses that word for). Each invoice's own `notes` states which published rule it follows. Wired into the concierge desk's Invoices view as "Raise, per your own published terms," a service dropdown priced straight from the catalogue, sitting beside the existing free-text drafting card rather than replacing it.

Verified against real catalogue prices, not assumed: Eyes On It (£125, under £200) raised one invoice at the full catalogue price; Deposit Protection Check (£249) raised two, £124.50 each, summing back to the original £249 exactly.

## 2026-09-01 · The Quote Kickoff Pack (small pack) gained the review step it was built without

Founder's own question, live, after the WhatsApp test: "I never saw when the small pack was issued for review." True, and by design as first built: a guardrail-clean `quote_pack_drafts` row at `status = 'ready'` went straight into a worker's own quote form (`QuotePanel.tsx`) the moment it finished drafting, no human step anywhere - unlike the big Kickoff Pack, which has exactly this review step (`yaad-kickoff-check`'s own Phase 2, `link_kickoff_draft_to_job()` as the manual door). Founder's own instruction: build the same review step here.

Mirrored the big pack's shape closely (`20260901r`). `quote_pack_drafts` gains `approved`, `approved_by`, `approved_at`. `yaad-quote-pack-check` gains a Phase 2: a finished, guardrail-clean draft is approved automatically, system-attributed, same wording as the big pack; a dirty one is left at `ready`, visible in a new concierge desk view, Quote Pack Drafts, for a human to clear or replace. `approve_quote_pack_draft()` is the manual door, admin only, refuses outright on any guardrail flag exactly like `link_kickoff_draft_to_job()` - no override, ever - and attributes the approval to the actual signed-in admin's email rather than a generic string.

**RLS is the real gate, not the page.** The read policy that let a worker see a draft at all previously had no status filter beyond the job qualifying; it now requires `status = 'approved'`, so an unreviewed draft is invisible to a worker's own query regardless of what `QuotePanel.tsx`'s client-side `usableDraft()` checks - which was updated to match, but is a courtesy now, not the boundary, the same "RLS is the real protection" principle CLAUDE.md states everywhere else. `web/app/jobs/page.tsx`'s own draft query and per-job dedup preference updated to prefer `approved` over `ready` for the same reason.

Verified live: manually triggered `yaad-quote-pack-check` against the real backlog of drafts sitting from before this migration - two approved cleanly, correctly attributed to the system, zero held, zero failures.

## 2026-09-01 · The first genuinely real WhatsApp test of the night found three more bugs no amount of simulated testing had caught

Everything above this point in tonight's Kickoff Pack rework was verified by calling the real database functions directly - real logic, real data, but never a message actually relayed by Twilio. The founder gave her own real WhatsApp number and ran the client and worker journeys for real. Three real bugs surfaced in the first four messages, all in `yaad-inbound`, none of them visible from the database side.

**1. Block order.** The new Kickoff Pack confirm block sat AFTER the existing booking block, and both recognise the identical reply (a bare job code). Her first real message, confirming her side for the first time, got intercepted by the booking block instead and correctly refused ("choose unlocks once this worker's Kickoff Pack is confirmed by both sides") - her own confirmation was never recorded. Fixed by moving the confirm block first.

**2. An ORM footgun, not a logic bug.** The candidate query used PostgREST's embedded-resource filter (`.select("...,jobs!inner(...)")`.`.not("jobs.client_phone", "is", null)`), which resolved in a shape the code's own `.map()` did not expect and silently produced zero matches. Nothing errored; the reply just fell through to the booking block again, with the exact same wrong refusal. Rewritten as plain queries joined by hand in JS, the same style already used successfully everywhere else in this file - this was the only block that had reached for an embed.

**3. Already-confirmed packs were still "candidates."** Once both sides had genuinely confirmed, the identical reply kept matching the confirm block anyway (it never checked whether there was anything left to confirm), so `agree_kickoff_pack_via_whatsapp()` correctly refused a third time ("No Kickoff Pack on this job is waiting on your confirmation") and the reply still never reached booking. Fixed by excluding, per side, any pack this phone has already confirmed at its current revision from the candidate list.

**Verified for real after each fix**, not assumed: her actual replies moved through confirm (client), book, and confirm (worker, on a second test job, same physical phone, correctly attributed as a different party) - all three read back from the database as genuinely recorded, not simulated.

**A fourth issue, copy not logic, caught by the founder mid-test:** the "both sides have signed off" message told the client nothing had gone wrong but never said what to do next - choosing is a second, separate reply, not automatic. Fixed: the client's copy of that message now says so directly; the worker's does not, since choosing is the client's action alone.

## 2026-09-01 · kickoff_packs.quote_id made unique, after a manual trigger raced pg_cron and produced two packs for one quote

Setting up a real test for the founder to run over her own WhatsApp number, manually invoking `yaad-kickoff-check` to speed a draft along landed in the same window as pg_cron's own minute-tick. Phase 2 reads every `ready`, unlinked draft once per invocation and inserts a pack for each; two invocations close enough together both read before either writes, so the same quote got two `kickoff_packs` rows with two different confirm codes. A real risk beyond testing, since the concierge desk can also trigger this by hand. Fixed with `unique (quote_id)` (`20260901q`): one quote, one pack, ever - revisions update `rev` in place on the same row, they never insert a second one, so the constraint costs nothing real and turns the race into an already-handled insert failure instead of a silent duplicate.

---

## 2026-09-01 · The WhatsApp booking door itself was broken by the Kickoff Pack rework, found running the exact test it was built for

Founder's own instruction: test the client and worker journey for real, over WhatsApp, not simulated through the portal. Doing that surfaced two more real, load-bearing defects, both direct fallout from the quote/pack rework earlier the same night (`20260901f`), neither caught by that migration's own testing because that testing used the portal's RPCs, never the WhatsApp doors.

**`sync_job_status()` didn't know `kickoff_requested` existed.** Its own "does this job have a live quote" check only ever looked for `status = 'submitted'`; a quote that had moved to `kickoff_requested` made the job read `open_for_quotes` again, as if nothing had happened. Not just a wrong label: `choose_worker_via_whatsapp()`'s own candidate query in `yaad-inbound` filters `jobs.status = 'quoted'` directly, so every job actually mid-flow through the new process - which is every job with a requested Kickoff Pack - was invisible to the WhatsApp booking door. Fixed (`20260901l`) to count `kickoff_requested` alongside `submitted`.

**`choose_worker_via_whatsapp()` itself was left checking two things that can no longer both be true at once.** It only searched for a quote at `status = 'submitted'` (the same fault as above, in its own query this time) and its own worker-readiness pre-check still read and wrote `scope_agreements`, the mechanism `_do_choose_worker()` stopped consulting the moment its gate became the Kickoff Pack's `both_confirmed_at`. The prior migration's own comment predicted a stale pre-check here would "fail open... caught one call further in"; live testing showed it does not - it returns `PENDING_WORKER_SCOPE` and never reaches `_do_choose_worker()` at all, a confident wrong answer rather than an honest refusal. Rebuilt (`20260901m`) to search `submitted` or `kickoff_requested` and defer entirely to `_do_choose_worker()` for whether booking is allowed, dropping the dead pre-check and the `PENDING_WORKER_SCOPE` branch in `yaad-inbound` along with it.

**Verified afterward against a real booking, start to finish, through the WhatsApp-native doors specifically** (`choose_worker_via_whatsapp`, `approve_stage_via_whatsapp`), not their portal siblings tested earlier: quote requested, Kickoff Pack drafted and confirmed by both sides, booked by phone-matched reply, arrival logged, evidence filed, stage approved by phone-matched reply. All correct. The one link still not exercised through a literal signed Twilio webhook, same caveat as the Kickoff Pack confirm work above.

---

## 2026-09-01 · Confirming a Kickoff Pack moved off the portal onto WhatsApp, for the worker

Founder's own correction, live, testing the journey end to end after the quote/pack rework below: "this need to go through whatsapp, it should not be done through the portal." CLAUDE.md already said so, written before that same night: the worker's whole surface is WhatsApp by design, and the web is only for onboarding and file upload. The Kickoff Pack rework a few hours earlier gave the worker a "Confirm as the worker" portal button anyway, exactly the surface that rule exists to prevent.

`agree_kickoff_pack()`'s core (record the agreement, check for both, stamp `both_confirmed_at`) moved into `_do_agree_kickoff_pack(pack_id, side, email)`, shared by the portal path and a new one, `agree_kickoff_pack_via_whatsapp(p_job, p_phone)` (`20260901i`, corrected twice more the same night, below). `yaad-inbound` gained a new job-code-reply lane, same family as `choose_worker_via_whatsapp` and `approve_stage_via_whatsapp`: either side's phone, matched against every job with an approved pack still waiting on that side, replies with the code to confirm theirs. `yaad-notify-client`'s `kickoff_pack_ready` message now says so directly instead of only linking to the portal. The worker-facing "Confirm as the worker" button is gone from both `/pack` and the job room's own early worker view; both now read "reply to Yaadly's WhatsApp message with `<job id>`" instead. The client keeps their portal button, since the client's primary channel was never in question.

**Two real bugs in the new function, both caught live testing it, neither from guessing:**

1. `agree_kickoff_pack_via_whatsapp(p_phone)`'s own `RETURNS TABLE(..., job_id text, pack_id text)` column names silently became variables inside the function body, PL/pgSQL's own well-known trap, and collided with a CTE returning columns of the identical bare names - "column reference pack_id is ambiguous" the first time it actually ran. Fixed (`20260901j`) by qualifying every CTE column reference.

2. Worse, and only found because the same worker genuinely had two Kickoff Packs pending at once by the time this got tested (one real, left over from before tonight's rework; one from the test itself): the function took only a phone number and searched globally for "the one pending pack", so replying with the EXACT correct code for one job still refused with "more than one is waiting", discarding the one piece of information - which job the reply actually named - that `yaad-inbound` had already worked out via `matchApprovingJob()` one line earlier. Every other WhatsApp door in this codebase takes the job it already matched as a parameter; this one tried to re-derive it from the phone alone. Rebuilt (`20260901k`) to the same shape as `choose_worker_via_whatsapp(p_job, p_phone)`: takes the job, checks only whether THIS job has a pending confirmation for this phone, refuses only then. Verified afterward against the exact failing case: a worker with two genuinely pending packs, confirming the named one correctly while the other stays untouched.

**Not verified: a real, Twilio-signature-signed webhook hitting this in production.** Every check above went through the underlying database function directly, with the real test accounts, and a careful re-read of the new `yaad-inbound` matching code. The Twilio signature check (`checkTwilioSignature`, reading `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN`) is real and cannot be faked without that secret, which correctly never leaves Supabase's own environment. Confirming this from an actual WhatsApp message needs a phone number really on file for a test account, or the founder testing it from her own number against a real job.

---

## 2026-09-01 · Accepting a quote no longer books it. A client can accept more than one, compare Kickoff Packs, and choose

Found live, testing the client journey end to end: pressing Accept on a quote set `jobs.worker_email` and lit the job up as `in_progress` immediately, before any scope of work or payment terms existed. The Kickoff Pack that carries both was drafted afterward, tied only to the job, with nothing pointing a client at it and nothing gating anything on it. Founder's own correction, live: "Accepting the job should not accept the job because there is no scope of work agreed and there's no payment terms agreed so it never should have gone on the portal," followed by the actual model wanted: "a client can accept many quotes and let the worker showcase their split with the kickoff pack and the client will then have the final say on what quote they will choose as they now have documents against a quote."

**The unit that gets confirmed moved from the job to the quote**, because more than one quote can now be live for the same job at once. `job_quotes` gained a status between `submitted` and `accepted`: `kickoff_requested`. `kickoff_drafts` and `kickoff_packs` gained `quote_id`, so a job can carry more than one draft or pack in flight, one per worker whose price the client wants to see terms against (`20260901f`).

**`accept_quote_as_me()` is retired**, replaced by `request_kickoff_as_me(p_quote)`: it flips a submitted quote to `kickoff_requested` and touches nothing on `jobs`. It can be called again for a different quote on the same job, on purpose. `yaad-kickoff-check`'s first phase now polls `job_quotes.status = 'kickoff_requested'` instead of "a job with a chosen worker," one request per quote, and `yaad-kickoff` accepts an optional `quoteId` to stamp the draft.

**`_do_choose_worker()`, the one real booking path under `choose_worker()` and `choose_worker_via_whatsapp()`, is regated onto the Kickoff Pack instead of `scope_agreements`.** `scope_agreements` was already known not to fit more than one live quote, since it carries one row per side per job, not per quote; `choose_worker_via_whatsapp()` already refuses outright the moment more than one quote is open, for exactly that reason. The Kickoff Pack can tell two workers' agreements apart, because it is drafted and confirmed per `quote_id`. The gate is now: this quote's Kickoff Pack has `both_confirmed_at` set. `scope_agreements` and `agreeScope()` are left in the schema, unread by the gate; the portal's separate scope tick-box UI is fully retired in this pass (`/portal/jobs/[id]` no longer renders it), rather than left standing next to a mechanism it no longer feeds.

**Four downstream places assumed booking happened before or alongside a Kickoff Pack, and broke the moment that stopped being true (`20260901g`):** `agree_kickoff_pack()` matched the worker side of a confirmation against `jobs.worker_email`, blank pre-booking, so a worker could never confirm their own pack; fixed to check the pack's own `quote_id` first. RLS on `jobs` ("workers can read their own jobs"), `kickoff_packs` ("parties read approved packs") and `kickoff_pack_agreements` all keyed off `jobs.worker_email` too, so an unbooked quoting worker could not even load the job row their own portal pages depend on; all three extended to also match via `job_quotes.worker_user`. `job_quotes`' own client-read policy only allowed `submitted` and `accepted`, so a client's own requested quote would have vanished from their own portal view the moment it moved to `kickoff_requested`. `notify_worker_kickoff_pack_ready()` and `yaad-notify-client`'s `kickoff_pack_ready` handling resolved the worker and picked "whichever pack last updated" by job id alone, both wrong once two packs can be in flight on one job; both now carry and read `quoteId`.

**Portal UI**: `/portal/jobs/[id]` gained an early, separate render for a worker with a live quote but no booking, since the rest of that page assumes a booked worker everywhere it says `role === "worker"` (arrival log, materials store) and none of that applies yet; rather than audit every section for a state that could not previously exist, an unbooked quoting worker gets a small focused view of their own quote and their own Kickoff Pack instead. The client's own quote cards gained a "Get a Kickoff Pack for this price" button per submitted quote, pack status once requested, and "Choose X for the job" once that pack is confirmed by both sides. `/pack` and `/pack/[doc]` take an optional `?quote=` and, failing that, match the confirm code from the link, rather than assuming the job's latest-updated pack is the right one. `AcceptPanel.tsx` and `/portal/join` updated to call `request_kickoff_as_me` and describe what it actually does.

**Separately, the same live test caught why a kickoff draft can fail at all**: `yaad-kickoff`'s `draftPart()` had no diagnostic logging on a parse failure, same class of silent failure fixed in `yaad-inbound` two entries below. `console.error` added, logging `finish_reason` and the first 1200 characters of the raw model response. The specific draft that prompted this (`JOB-WA-1788258939807`, part A1, "did not return usable JSON") had already self-healed on `yaad-kickoff-check`'s own retry three minutes later, so the exact cause of that one instance is still unconfirmed, but the next failure will say why.

**Caught running the actual test this was all built for, same evening:** `request_kickoff_as_me()` reported success and returned the job id on every call, but `job_quotes.status` never actually moved to `kickoff_requested`. `job_quotes_touch()` (a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger) exists specifically so a non-admin caller can only move a quote into an admin-only status through a transaction-local flag, `yaadly.choosing`, that `choose_worker()` and the retired `accept_quote_as_me()` both set around their own updates. `request_kickoff_as_me()`, written fresh in `20260901f`, never set it, so every call silently reverted itself. Live proof this class of bug is real, not theoretical: the RUNBOOK already documented the identical failure shape happening to `accept_quote_as_me()` before 31 Aug 2026. Fixed in `20260901h`, which also resets any quote caught mid-flight by the bug. Verified afterward end to end against production, live, no shortcuts: two real quotes on a fresh test job, both accepted, both packs drafted in parallel and auto-issued clean within about ninety seconds, both confirmed by the correct worker on each side (proving the pre-booking worker-match fix above), one chosen, the other auto-declined, the job booked correctly.

**Closed the same evening, founder asked directly:** a worker with a live, unbooked quote now appears on their own worker portal job list. `/portal/worker` and the door page both widen their worker filter with a direct `job_quotes` lookup (`worker_user = auth.uid()`) rather than `jobs.worker_email` alone, so the WhatsApp link is a shortcut into the job, not the only way in. The existing `WORKER_STATUS['quoted']` copy, "You have quoted, waiting on the client," needed no change; it was already honest about this exact state.

---

## 2026-09-01 · Police record check and the trial job dropped from worker vetting, founder correction

Founder read the marketing copy on `docs/marketplace.html` describing worker vetting and said plainly: "remove trial jobs, i dont do that" and "i no longer ask for [the police check]... like i remove that from my site and live site doesnt ask for that." Neither the JCF police record check nor an independent reviewer attending a worker's first job at Yaadly's cost is something the business actually does, even though both were built into the applicant form, the Worker Guidelines, the AI vetting review and the public marketing pages.

**Removed everywhere it was asked for or claimed.** The `police_check` document type and its upload path, the `policeStatus` field and the "8 · Trial job" step are gone from `JoinFlow.tsx`; the ON_THE_BOARD top tier text now reads "until your telephoned references are done" rather than naming a police check. `yaad-vetting-upload` no longer accepts a `police_check` document or writes `police_status`. `yaad-vetting-review` dropped `police_check` from `CORE_DOCS` and `DOC_LABEL`, the police check age computation, and the line telling the desk an applicant said they had no check yet. The concierge desk's vetting queue lost its "Police check" column, synced to `concierge-deploy`. The public worker profile no longer shows a "no current JCF police record check" warning. `docs/marketplace.html`, `docs/faq.html`, `preview/index.html`, the specs and `web/lib/hubspotConfig.ts`'s certification dropdown all had the same claim removed.

**The £500, occupied home, and keys threshold stays, gated on references alone.** That threshold is a real, database enforced gate (`enforce_vetted_worker_on_quote`, 20260831d), not marketing copy, and the founder did not ask to remove it, only the police check half of what unlocks it. A Probation worker is still refused those jobs until the three referees are telephoned; the trigger itself never actually checked `police_status`, only the desk's own decision to flip `vetting_state` to `verified` referenced it, in RUNBOOK.md and in the worker's own copy of what was expected of them.

**Worker Guidelines moved to v1.4** because the removed text was a signed legal document, not just a web page. `legal-copy.json`'s section 2 lost the police check callout and the trial job paragraph, gained a v1.4 amendment note saying plainly that both are removed because neither is part of how Yaadly actually vets a worker. `app_settings.worker_guidelines_version` bumped to match in a new migration (`20260901i`), because `current_doc_version('worker_guidelines')` reads that table, not the JSON file, and the job matching gate in `yaad_match` compares a worker's signed version against it exactly: leaving it at '1.3' would have failed every worker who signed the corrected text and passed every worker who signed the one just withdrawn.

**Not yet deployed.** The two edited edge functions (`yaad-vetting-upload`, `yaad-vetting-review`) need `supabase functions deploy` from disk, and the new migration needs applying, before any of this is live. Nothing in this pass touched Persona identity verification or the referee phone calls, those were not part of what the founder said was wrong.

Founder's own point, live, looking at the actual quotes page: "Book Test Worker (SEED)" reads as final agreement, and at that point in the journey there is no written scope of work on screen for either side to have actually read, only a price. Accepting a quote settles the price and books the worker provisionally; the Kickoff Pack that follows, confirmed separately by both sides, is where the real scope and payment terms are agreed. The button read as the whole thing when it is only the first half.

Changed in both places a client can do this: `AcceptPanel.tsx` (the no-account `/jobs/[id]/quotes` page) and the portal's own quote list (`/portal/jobs/[id]`). Button text changed from "Book X" to "Accept X's price"; a new line under each button states plainly that this is provisional and the Kickoff Pack is the actual agreement. No RPC or database behaviour changed, copy only: `accept_quote_as_me()` and `choose_worker()` already do exactly what the new copy describes, the old copy was the only thing overstating it.

## 2026-09-01 · A client got two different kinds of silence on WhatsApp, both real, found live against Twilio's own logs

Two separate real messages from the founder's phone got no useful reply. Neither was diagnosable from this project's own logs at the time, which is itself the first finding.

**Confirmed via Twilio's own message log (Monitor → Logs → Messaging), not assumed:** the first failure has an inbound message received with no outbound reply anywhere near it, ever, where every other exchange that day shows a reply within seconds. `yaad-inbound`'s outermost catch block returned a JSON 500 on any uncaught exception, and only ever called `root.recordError(e)`, which lands on the trace span and nowhere in `function_logs`. A client whose message hit that branch got silence, and nothing in this project said why. `console.error` added alongside the trace record; the actual exception for this specific incident was never captured, since the logging did not exist yet when it happened, but the next one will be.

**The second failure, on a fresh message minutes later, was root-caused directly from the new logging.** `readTheJob()`'s call to MiniMax carried `max_tokens: 1100`, the smallest budget of any model call in this codebase (every sibling call is 1200 or higher; `yaad-completion` is 8000). This model reasons inside a `<think>` block before writing its JSON answer, and that reasoning counts against the same token budget. Caught live: `readTheJob: no JSON object in model content: <think>\nThe client needs a plumber... I have enough information here:\n- What the work is:\n` — the response was cut off mid-thought, no closing `</think>` tag ever written, so `stripNoise()`'s regex could not strip it and `extractJson()` found no `{` to start from. `readTheJob()` correctly returned `null` rather than throwing, so the client did not get silence this time, they got the hardcoded generic opener ("Tell me what needs doing, which parish...") instead of an answer to what they had already said twice. Raised `max_tokens` to 2500.

Both fixes deployed and verified against the actual deployed source before either was trusted (`deno check`, then `supabase functions deploy yaad-inbound`, then re-read the live logs).

---

## 2026-09-01 · The Kickoff Pack gains client responsibilities, an itemised cost breakdown and a legal placeholders section

Founder supplied a reference quote template from another platform and asked for the useful shape adopted, explicitly not the whole thing: it used the word "escrow" repeatedly, a hard-banned term in this codebase (`guardrails.ts`/`guardrails.py`, "never say escrow, say held safely with a licensed payment provider"), and several sections read as real contract terms (warranty length, dispute process, who pulls permits) that belong to the founder and her solicitor, not to an automated document. Declined those two pieces explicitly before building anything, confirmed with her, then built the parts that were genuinely missing.

**`client_responsibilities` added to the model's own `scope_of_works` schema** in `yaad-kickoff`'s `SYSTEM` prompt, same quality bar as the existing included/excluded items: concrete to the property, never "cooperate with the contractor". Model-generated on purpose, since what a client needs to do differs job to job the same way the scope itself does.

**`cost_breakdown` and `terms_placeholders` are never asked of the model, added in code after the four-part draft merges.** `cost_breakdown` is built directly from `scope_of_works.included`, one row per scope item, materials/labour/total all blank: this keeps the row names identical to the scope the client is actually agreeing to and never risks a second prompt call answering rule 1 ("never price") differently under load. `terms_placeholders` is fixed text naming exactly what is still open (warranty, permits, dispute process, what happens if a hidden defect is found), each line explicit that it is not decided by Yaadly and needs the founder's solicitor before it becomes standard wording, same "question for an adviser, not an asserted answer" pattern this repository already uses for compliance items elsewhere.

**Portal rendering**: `packDocs.tsx` gained a "What you need to do" field on the scope page, a cost breakdown table on the payment page, and a new tenth pack page, "Terms to confirm". `concierge.html`'s `KICK_DOCS` list, which this file's own header comment says must name the same things the same way as the client view, updated to match. Both `yaad-kickoff` and the web app redeployed; `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` (47/47) both clean before either deploy.

## 2026-09-01 · Continuing the WhatsApp test: two real bugs, a real gap, and one wrong feature built then correctly reverted within the hour

Same founder-directed session as the entry below, continued: two full real conversations sent from her own phone, second one deliberately overlapping her first job's 12-hour thread window to test the boundary.

**A second, unrelated job sent within 12 hours of the first read back the first job's summary and never started gathering the second at all.** `const continuing` in `yaad-inbound/index.ts` matched purely on `channel + from_addr` within the window, with no regard for whether the prior thread had already finished. A reply about an already-done job (approving a stage, answering a comment) is intercepted by its own lane well before this check runs, so nothing legitimate needed the old behaviour. Fixed by also requiring `prior.stage !== "done"`; a done thread now starts fresh rather than swallowing whatever comes next. Found live because the founder's second real message, about an electrician, came back as a stale plumber-job summary.

**Deploying that fix broke every continuing reply outright, caught and fixed within minutes of shipping.** The same pass added `access_contact` to the row `yaad-inbound` writes (see entry below), written as `s(card?.access_note) || null`. `jobs.access_contact` is `NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`; the `|| null` sent an actual NULL over the wire the moment `card?.access_note` was empty, and every `UPDATE` on a continuing thread failed with Postgres `23502`, silently from the client's side: no reply at all. Fixed to `s(card?.access_note)`, which already returns `""`. Worth naming plainly: this was this session's own bug, in code deployed minutes earlier, caught by the founder saying "got no response back" and confirmed from `edge_logs` (`response.headers.proxy_status: "PostgREST; error=23502"`), not by any test suite.

**The model's own confirmation read missed an unambiguous "yes" once, live, and there was nothing under it to catch that.** `card?.confirmed` is one model call's classification of one message; the founder's actual final message, "Monique Sewell-Bennett, [email]. Yes, that's everything, go ahead.", came back `confirmed: false`. The job row still wrote (the row write is unconditional every turn), so it looked finished in the database while the conversation logic still thought it was waiting, and the client got a bare thank-you with no portal link, no explanation. Added a deterministic word-match backstop (`yes`, `go ahead`, `correct`, `confirmed`, and similar, tested against that turn's own raw text only) that can only ever add a `true` the model missed, never override one it read correctly, same shape `wantsOut()` already uses for "cancel". Separately, and independently worth doing: the "confirming" stage reply used to just ask an open question and hope the answer was recognisable. Founder's own point: a client should be told what to do, not left to guess the right words. The reply now always ends with an explicit "Reply yes if that is right, or just tell me what to change," appended in code so it is consistent every time rather than however the model happened to phrase the read-back.

**Also found and fixed while diagnosing the above: `readTheJob()`'s failure paths were invisible.** Every one of its `return null` branches (HTTP error, unparseable body, no JSON object in the model's content, malformed JSON) only ever called `sp.recordError()`, which lands on the trace span and does not appear in `function_logs`. A client who fell through to the hardcoded generic opener left no console trail at all explaining why. `console.error` added alongside the span record on every one of those paths. The generic fallback's own wording was also tightened at the founder's request, from "whereabouts the property is" to "which parish the property is in", matching the model prompt's own field name rather than a vaguer phrase, since parish is the literal column matching runs on.

**`client_go_live()` never opened a returning client's later job, and the existing recovery button couldn't reach it either, same root cause.** Full account: `sync_job_status()` sets a not-yet-open job to `'draft'`, not `'awaiting_client_setup'`, the moment the client is already cleared (signed guidelines on file from an earlier job). `client_go_live()`'s `WHERE` clause only ever matched `'awaiting_client_setup'`. A returning client's second job therefore reached `open = false, status = 'draft', client_email` correctly bound, and stayed there permanently: correct by every individual rule, invisible to every worker, and the founder's own "stuck job" recovery button (`web/app/portal/golive-actions.ts`, built 28 Aug for a different stuck case, materials store nominated out of order) checks the same `'awaiting_client_setup'` status so it could not reach this one either. Fixed by matching both statuses a not-yet-open, cleared client's job can honestly carry (`20260901b_client_go_live_missed_a_returning_clients_second_job.sql`). Found live: the founder's own second job, on a number that had already completed the whole flow once earlier the same session.

**A client-side "edit your description" feature was built, shipped, and correctly reverted within the hour, by the founder, on sight.** Asked for a way for a client to correct what a worker will read before the job goes live (`BoardPreview.tsx` already showed it, read only, with nothing to fix it if it was wrong), built as `edit_job_description()` in Postgres plus a server action and a component, same shape as `nominate_materials_store()`. Wrong the moment it shipped: `jobs.descr` is not a draft, it is the record, the raw transcript, "In their own words," and the system's own working notes on the job (whether an email is proven yet). The founder caught it immediately, on the live page, seeing the edit form pre-filled with exactly that transcript: "dont allow them to edit this, it is the actual log we need." Reverted within the hour: `edit_job_description()`'s grants revoked from every client-facing role (`postgres` and `service_role` only, confirmed directly against `information_schema.routine_privileges` rather than assumed), the component and server action deleted, the page reverted, redeployed. The function definition itself is kept, not dropped, so a later reader of the migrations in order sees what was tried and why it stopped, rather than the attempt vanishing. If an editable summary is wanted later, it is a new column and a new function, never this one re-granted.

Test data added this round, same conventions as the entry below: job `JOB-WA-1788224177580` (electrician, St Andrew), same founder phone number and email throughout.

## 2026-09-01 · A real end-to-end test of the client and worker journeys, three findings fixed

Run at the founder's request: post a job through the web wizard, and through a simulated WhatsApp conversation, and follow each as far as the system's real scope goes (no live payment integration exists yet, so that stopped at the record-keeping fields, not a transfer).

**The web wizard's guest flow never told the client how to finish.** `web/app/jobs/new/PostJob.tsx`'s "Send my job" step calls `yaad-enquiry`, built for the marketing site's plain contact form, which writes to the `enquiries` table with no `job_id` column at all. The job itself stays at `awaiting_client_setup` with `client_name`/`client_email`/`client_phone` blank, by design (`yaad-post-job`'s draft mode writes an anonymous row on purpose, so that possession of an email is never mistaken for proof of it). The completion mechanism, `/portal/join?job=…&code=…`, already existed and already worked, verified directly: `claim_code_as_me()` binds `client_email` to the job the moment the client verifies. What was missing was simpler than it looked: the confirmation screen never showed the client that link or their `portal_code`, which the draft response already returns and always has. Fixed by surfacing both on the "Job received" screen and folding the link into the enquiry message, so it also reaches the receipt email and the desk copy. No account-creation logic changed; this is presentation of what already existed.

**The concierge desk's own safety net for this exact situation was reading a dead column, or so it appeared at the time.** `concierge/concierge.html`'s "Built a job, never signed" (Intake) panel filtered on `client_user is null`. Grepped clean across every migration and function in the repository at the time: nothing wrote it. That was true of the repository and false of the live database, corrected further down this same entry once the reason surfaced. Switched the filter (three call sites, plus the badge count and the overview health check) to `status = 'awaiting_client_setup'`, which is the column `client_go_live()` itself checks before it will open a job, and which turned out to be the better choice anyway: it is versioned, actively maintained, and authoritative, where `client_user` is set by a trigger this repository did not know it had. The panel change stands.

**The WhatsApp testing above was against the wrong function, corrected same session.** `yaad-whatsapp-webhook` speaks to Meta's Cloud API directly and has not received real traffic since `0e654f6`, 31 Aug 2026, "Move WhatsApp evidence intake from Meta to Twilio, same day", founder's own words there: "remove meta from this moving forward." That commit ported evidence intake to `yaad-inbound` and explicitly reverted `yaad-whatsapp-webhook` to its pre-evidence-intake state rather than leave it "dead code nobody will ever configure" — but that earlier state still included a full guided, one-question-at-a-time client intake (`finalizeIntake()`, built 29 Aug per its own comments), which the revert left standing. Untouched since, still deployed, still reachable only by a Meta send that will never arrive. This session found it, tested it as if it were live, and fixed two real bugs in it (`trade` never written to the column `yaad-match` routes on; no admin notification on a completed job) before realising the mistake. Corrected here rather than pretended away: those fixes were real, but the file itself was never the live path, so they fixed nothing anybody would hit.

**The actual live path, `yaad-inbound`, was already correct on both counts this session initially flagged as missing.** Its own new-job intake (thread-based across `intake_threads`, the model deciding `gathering` → `confirming` → `done` rather than a fixed seven-question script) already writes `trade`/`trade_source` on the insert. Its `notifyAdmin()` already fires on the first message of a new thread and again the moment a job is confirmed, which is a stronger guarantee than what was added to the dead Meta file: the founder is told a lead exists before it is even a real job, not only once it becomes one. No code changed here; this is what was actually found on rereading `yaad-inbound` properly after the correction above.

**What is still genuinely unverified, stated plainly.** `yaad-inbound` requires a valid `X-Twilio-Signature`, computed with `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN`, a secret this session does not hold and should not try to reproduce. Every piece of logic that does not need a real signed request was traced directly against the source and the live database; the first genuinely signed message from a real device is the proof that remains, same limitation every prior WhatsApp-via-Twilio entry in this log has stated honestly rather than assumed past.

**`yaad-whatsapp-webhook` was deleted outright, founder's instruction, same session: "strip it," then "remove it from the dead file and delete the dead file afterwards."** Not reverted to an earlier state this time, the way the evidence half was on 31 Aug: removed entirely, source and deployed function both (`supabase functions delete yaad-whatsapp-webhook --project-ref leffyisvfvjwzilydlwf`). The worker-signup-via-WhatsApp lane living in the same file went with it, unreviewed and untested this session, flagged to the founder as a separate, smaller instance of the identical problem before she gave the instruction to remove the whole file rather than pick it apart. Every doc that named the function as a live thing (`CLAUDE.md`, `web/README.md`, `concierge/README.md`, `supabase/functions/README.md`, `specs/ARCHITECTURE.md`) was corrected in the same pass to point at `yaad-inbound` instead, or to say plainly that the pointer needs rechecking where this session could not confirm the replacement (`specs/PORTALS-BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.4, `specs/PORTAL-SPEC.md`, on which function carries a client's portal chat message out to a worker's WhatsApp: the inbound half of that relay is confirmed live in `yaad-inbound`, the outbound half was not reverified before this entry was written). Nothing about this removal touched `yaad-inbound` itself, which was already correct and is the only WhatsApp path this repository has now.

Test data left in the live database, all under names beginning `TEST QA`, all pointed at the founder's own email as contact: jobs `JOB-WEB-1788219235030`, `JOB-WEB-1788220315236`, `JOB-WA-1788220553320`, `JOB-WA-1788220705262`, `JOB-WA-1788220885058`; enquiries `732aa3d1-…`, `b434f8b3-…`; application `APP-35885A`. Left in place rather than deleted; nobody was asked to remove them.

**The real, live, signed test the founder then insisted on, correctly, proved the loop end to end.** Everything above was code tracing and unsigned synthetic payloads. Asked to prove it for real, the founder sent an actual WhatsApp message from her own phone (+447767171858) through a clean, fresh conversation: opener, access and photo question, name and email and confirmation, portal link.
