It is opt-in through `MINIMAX_API_KEY`, which is currently unset, so as of this paragraph the code carries the fallback and the estate does not use it. And it logs a line naming the country on every call it is chosen for, so the question "which messages went there" has an answer in `function_logs` without tracing being on. `textmodel_test.ts` asserts all three.

**The privacy page moved in the same commit.** It named Mistral and said MiniMax was the past. A fallback that can fire is not the past, so the paragraph and the table row now say: fallback only, when Mistral is not configured, China, logged. Disclosing it on the day is the difference between a decision and a leak.

**Reach.** Only a function redeployed with the new `_shared/textmodel.ts` copy carries the branch. `yaad-sketch` and `yaad-report` were deployed on the day; the other seven text callers keep the no-fallback copy until someone deploys them, which the runbook says.

`supabase/functions/_shared/textmodel.ts`, `textmodel_test.ts`, `docs/privacy.html`, `RUNBOOK.md` §9.

## The file is produced even when Mistral is rate limited (6 Sep 2026)

**The instruction.** "I want my file to be produced even when the limit is used on Mistral." Said the morning after the fallback went back in with the condition that a present-but-failing Mistral key would never fall through. That condition is now narrower: a 400 still never falls through, a rate limit or a server error can.

**What was actually wrong.** `yaad-report` and `yaad-sketch` assembled their files with a bare `fetch`. Not `fetchModel`, which had carried a retry with `Retry-After` since the 4th; a bare call. One 429 from Mistral's free tier, which allows about one request a second across nine functions sharing one key, and the desk got a 502 and no file. The failover was the request, but the retry was most of the fix: a per-second limit clears in a second, and nothing was waiting that second.

**What changed.** `fetchModel` takes `retries` and `maxRetryWaitMs`, defaulting to the webhook's one retry and four seconds so nothing that already used it changed. `chatWithFailover()` wraps it: Mistral, then up to two retries honouring `Retry-After` to fifteen seconds, and then, only if the answer is still a 429 or a 5xx and only if `MINIMAX_API_KEY` is set, one call to MiniMax, logged with the reason. The provider that actually answered comes back with the response, so the span carries the region the text really went to rather than the one that was tried first. The two desk callers use it. The webhook callers do not, and should not: a client's WhatsApp message is not a file, and fifteen seconds is the whole budget.

**A refused key fails over too, from the same day.** The Mistral key on the project started returning `401 Invalid API Key` on the night of the 5th, and Monique's instruction was "use the MiniMax key when Mistral fails". A 401 is not a 400: the request is fine, the credential is refused, and that says nothing about whether another provider will take it. So `failsOver()` names 429, 401, 403 and 5xx, a 401 goes across without a retry because it is the same answer every time, and a 400 still never does. `secrets list` showed both `MINIMAX_API_KEY` and `MISTRAL_API_KEY` written at 23:22 on the 5th, so the fallback was armed by her own hand the night before this paragraph; the sentence below about it being unset was true when written and is not now.

**Today's state, as first written.** `MINIMAX_API_KEY` is unset, on Monique's instruction not to touch secrets. So what is live is the retry, which on its own turns a short rate limit into a slightly slower file. The China path exists in code and in the privacy page and moves nothing until that one secret is set. Proved four ways in `textmodel_test.ts`: retries honoured, no key means no reroute, key means one reroute after the retries, and a 400 never reroutes.

`supabase/functions/_shared/textmodel.ts`, `textmodel_test.ts`, `supabase/functions/yaad-report/index.ts`, `supabase/functions/yaad-sketch/index.ts`, `docs/privacy.html`, `RUNBOOK.md` §9.

## The sketch pack becomes a site inspection report, and learns what a real video looks like (6 Sep 2026)

**Tested for real, with a real video, for the first time.** Monique filmed seven seconds of one bedroom on an iPhone and tried to build a pack. Every layer that had been reasoned about in August met a real file and most of them failed, one after another, in a single morning. The fixes are listed in the order the failures appeared, because each one was invisible until the one before it was cleared.

1. **The stills were blank.** Her browser could open the .mov but not decode it: iPhones record HEVC by default and not every browser has the codec. The page drew twelve black squares and said nothing. Now: MP4 only, enforced by the file picker and by a check on the name, with the fix written where she reads it (send the video to yourself on WhatsApp and use the copy it sends back, which is an MP4). A still that comes out black is detected and reported instead of sent. `preload` went from `auto` to `metadata`, because the whole video was being pulled into memory before the first still.
2. **NVIDIA's 90b vision model answered nothing in forty seconds, twice.** The sketch job now defaults to the 11b, the same model already reading evidence photos; `NVIDIA_SKETCH_MODEL` moves it back without a deploy. The desk sends one still per call, down from six and then three, so a slow answer costs one still and not the batch.
3. **The 11b read the system prompt back as its answer.** Llama 3.2 vision does not honour a system message next to an image. The instructions now travel inside the user message ahead of the image. Same words, different envelope.
4. **The 11b describes the room correctly but does not always wrap the answer as JSON.** The function extracts the first object in the answer, and if there is none, asks once more at temperature zero with a strict instruction. The desk carries on past a still that still fails, tries the failures once more at the end, and marks anything left as "not described" rather than stopping the run.
5. **One room came back as two.** The model named the same bedroom two ways from two angles. Three fixes: the room name under each still can be typed over; stills can be selected and named as a group, with the confirmation shown three ways; and the assembler enforces the person's names in code, merging rooms with the same name and collapsing to exactly one room when every still names one. The prompt says the names are final, and the code makes sure.
6. **The pack builder read MiniMax's thinking as its answer.** MiniMax-M2.7 reasons in a `<think>` block before answering. `answerText()` and `firstJsonObject()` in `_shared/textmodel.ts` strip it and take the first object; the assembler and the report drafter both use them, both set `max_tokens` high enough that the answer survives the thinking, and the assembler asks once more strictly if it still is not JSON.
7. **Thirty-six stills of one bedroom is thirty-six chances to be wrong.** Eight by default, sixteen at most, and never closer than one still every three seconds of video.
8. **The cross removes a still** rather than greying it out with a plus to put it back, because a greyed still still on the page reads as still in the pack.

**Then the document changed shape, on her instruction.** It is titled **Site Inspection Report**, it carries the property address, and it shows a map of the property from Google's Static Maps. Two deliberate August defaults were overridden by that: the pack held no address, and no third party but NVIDIA and the text model saw anything from it. Now the address is stored on the pack (`address`, `map_image`), the map is fetched once by `yaad-sketch` with a server-side key and stored as a data URI so the client's document never calls Google itself, and `docs/privacy.html` names Google Maps and the United States. It needs `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` set as a secret; without it the button says so and does nothing. The indicative stamp is unchanged: the map places a marker by address, not by survey, and the document still measures nothing.
